# Management-Governance Module — NU (Synthesis)

## Abstract

Nu appears operationally competent but only partly aligned with outside shareholders. Capital allocation is mixed: diluted EPS rose 175.6% from FY23 to FY25 and a US$500.4 million buyback reduced end-period issued shares 0.5%, yet returns on acquisitions and reinvestment are not disclosed. Broad employee equity and a three-year clawback help, but individual executive scorecards are opaque and insiders were net open-market sellers. The central risk is founder David Vélez’s 74.4% voting control through 20-for-1 Class B shares while serving as CEO and chair. Clean audited opinions and no hard disqualifier keep the call from being negative, but limited legal and network sweeps leave the verdict Standard / mixed.

## 1. Stewardship Verdict

- **Verdict:** Standard / mixed
- **NON-NEGOTIABLE GATE: PASS** — no hard disqualifier, no Critical red flag, no Disqualifying-graded controller/CEO/CFO/chair, and no material undisclosed legal matter. The checklist answered 114 of 179 registry items.
- **Hard disqualifier flagged (business-model/01)?** N — the scan found no trigger across its eight tests. [Business-Model Disqualifier Scan — NU, §§1–3, 2026-08-30]
- Management quality /100: **68**
- Capital allocation /100: **65**
- Incentive alignment /100: **52**
- Shareholder friendliness /100: **46**
- Disclosure candor /100: **68**
- People & network integrity /100: **55** (raw 58; capped from the query-budget shortfall)
- Audit & assurance quality /100: **53**
- RPT & leakage risk /100, **higher = worse:** **36**
- Contingent-liability risk /100, **higher = worse:** **27**
- Accounting-forensics risk /100, **higher = worse:** **29**
- Legal & regulatory risk /100, **higher = worse:** **40**
- Governance risk /100, **higher = worse:** **55** — synthesis judgment anchored to founder entrenchment and two unique formal red flags, offset by no proven control abuse, leakage, hard disqualifier, or gate failure.
- Data quality /100: **74**
- Overall usefulness /100: **72**
- **Checklist coverage:** **114/179 (63.7%)** — 31 Green / 77 Amber / 6 Red / 65 N-A (47 Insufficient Data and 18 Not Applicable)
- Insider ownership: CIQ reports 22.80% insider ownership; the filing attributes 18.6% economic ownership and 74.4% voting power to Vélez. Open-market activity was 0 buys and 4 sales, net −592,707 shares. [FY25 Form 20-F, Item 7A, pp.202–203; CIQ Ownership and Insider Trading exports, through 2026-08-26 — vendor]
- Biggest governance signal: one founder is CEO and chair, controls 74.4% of votes through 20:1 Class B shares, and holds nomination and consent rights; this is entrenchment, not evidence of misconduct. [FY25 Form 20-F, Items 6A, 7A and 10B]
- **Checklist Risk:** max(36 RPT, 27 contingent-liability, 29 accounting-forensics, 40 legal/regulatory) = **40**, bound by legal/regulatory coverage.
- **Governance Score /100:** 0.14×65 + 0.11×52 + 0.11×46 + 0.10×68 + 0.11×68 + 0.09×53 + 0.11×55 + 0.11×(100−55) + 0.12×(100−40) = **57.13**, reported as **57**.
- **Confidence-Adjusted Governance Score /100:** 57.13 × 68% = **38.85**, reported as **39**.
- **Governance Rating:** **Watchlist**
- **Confidence Score /100:** **68** — primary filings are strong, but legal/person/entity sweeps are incomplete and two upstream output-schema defects required conservative reconciliation.
- **Red-Flag Count / Critical Red-Flag Count:** **2 / 0** unique canonical flags.

## 1A. Module Disconfirmation

- **Strongest bear point:** Vélez’s 18.6% economic stake carries 74.4% of votes, a 55.8-point wedge, while he is both CEO and chair; public Class A holders cannot independently replace the board or block major actions. [FY25 Form 20-F, Items 6A, 7A and 10B]
- **Strongest bull point:** FY23–FY25 consolidated audits were unqualified, FY25 internal control over financial reporting was effective, disclosed non-audit fees were 7.8% of audit fees, and the hard-disqualifier scan found no trigger. [FY25 Form 20-F, auditor report and Item 16C; Business-Model Disqualifier Scan — NU, §1]
- **Single killer risk:** the controller could use nomination, consent, dilution or transaction powers against Class A holders; no abuse is proven today, but minority blocking power is weak. [FY25 Form 20-F, Items 6A and 10B]
- **Disconfirming evidence already visible:** fully independent Audit/Risk membership, a lead independent director, group-owned core trademarks, tiny known director-linked balances and no proven leakage argue against treating structural control as present misconduct. [FY25 Form 20-F, Items 6A and Note 28; INPI process 925913570, 2024-10-15]

The six registry Reds are not six unique canonical red flags. A4-02 and A4-13 are two checklist views of one RF-AUD-007 event. A1-02, A1-03, A3-03 and A3-04 are structural Red tests for combined roles and voting asymmetry; the owner supplied no canonical RF ID, so this synthesis does not invent one.

## 3. Reconciliation

- **Economic ownership versus minority alignment:** 04 records meaningful insider economics, but the 55.8-point control/economic wedge and 20:1 votes make the more conservative 05 minority-rights read decisive. Shareholder friendliness remains 46, not a positive score derived from ownership alone. [FY25 Form 20-F, Item 7A]
- **Independent board labels versus control:** 7 of 9 official directors are designated independent and Audit/Risk is fully independent, but the controller determines ordinary votes and has special nomination/consent rights. Formal independence does not cancel entrenchment. [FY25 Form 20-F, Items 6A and 10B]
- **Candor versus adjusted framing:** management’s Q1 timing bridge was direct and later matched Q2, but recurring stock-compensation exclusion lifts FY25 adjusted profit by US$203.7m, or 7.1%. This supports a mixed 68 rather than a high-trust read. [Q1/Q2 2026 calls; FY25 Form 20-F, Item 5A]
- **Clean issuer audits versus audit-firm discipline:** NU’s opinions and FY25 ICFR are clean; the PCAOB order concerns other issuer audits. RF-AUD-007 therefore lowers assurance quality without asserting a defect in NU’s audit. [FY25 Form 20-F, auditor report; PCAOB Release 105-2025-008, 2025-03-11]
- **Small quantified contingencies versus large unallocated claim:** the US$54.018m legal-exposure build is 0.41% of net worth, but the COP9.3tn GMF claim is an all-defendant amount across 52 entities and cannot be assigned to Nu. It stays an Amber uncertainty, not a fabricated pro-rata liability. [H1 FY26 Interim Report, Note 25; FY25 Form 20-F, Item 8A]
- **Cash-flow optics versus bank accounting:** H1 FY26 CFO was negative, but the non-financial CFO/PAT and Beneish/Dechow tests are invalid for a deposit-funded lender. The bank overlay’s +149bp ECL-intensity rise is the comparable warning; no manipulation flag fires. [H1 FY26 Interim Report; Management-Governance MODULE_RULES, financials overlay]
- **People cap:** 07 reported 58 and cited only max-65/max-70 caps. Its Scope-Boundary register, however, labels several E-A branches budget_exhausted, including founder/KMP-controlled holder vehicles. The exact rule applies max 55, so synthesis uses 55. [People & Network Integrity Dossiers — NU, §§0 and 5; MODULE_RULES, query-budget exhaustion cap]
- **Registry duplication:** A6-03, A6-09, A9-01 and A9-09 had multiple owner rows. The checklist uses the owner’s primary aggregate row and retains every detail row in §2A and governance_findings.csv.

**Forensic tag propagation:** RF-DISC-002 (recurring “one-off” / aggressive non-GAAP add-backs) fired and is carried below. RF-AUD-007 also fired. No RF-MGT-005, RF-DISC-001 or RF-REG-002 fired.

## 5. Stewardship Summary

Management has met the small set of mature operating promises, and diluted earnings per share rose 175.6% from FY23 to FY25, but the evidence cannot attribute that gain to each capital use. The new buyback reduced end-period shares, while the weighted diluted count still rose 0.2% because purchases began late; full-year per-share delivery remains open. Broad employee equity and a clawback point toward ownership, but undisclosed executive scorecards leave it unclear whether pay rewards returns or merely continued service and growth. Minority holders have weak practical protection because the founder controls 74.4% of votes and is CEO and chair, although independent committees, clean issuer audits and no proven leakage are meaningful offsets. The main reason for caution is therefore structural control combined with incomplete external integrity/legal coverage, not a proven fraud or related-party extraction.

| RF-AUD-007 | Audit firm regulator discipline | High (non-gate) | PCAOB censured KPMG Brazil, imposed a US$700,000 penalty and required remediation for other issuer audits; NU was not named | PCAOB Release 105-2025-008, 2025-03-11 | Audit quality 53; two checklist Reds, one unique flag | Obtain Audit Committee/KPMG evidence on remediation and whether affected systems touched NU. |

Red-flag count: **2 unique**. Critical: **0**. RF-DISC-002’s upstream Medium qualifier is preserved; it is not promoted into a gate flag. RF-AUD-007 is one event even though A4-02 and A4-13 both test it.

## 6. What Would Change The Stewardship Verdict?

| Current Verdict | What Would Strengthen It | What Would Weaken It | Data Needed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Standard / mixed | A sunset or Class-A/minority gate on super-votes; named return-based pay weights and outcomes; full-year buyback reducing diluted shares; complete person/entity sweeps with no material fact; clean KPMG remediation evidence | Controller-favored dilution/RPT/M&A; unexplained gatekeeper exit; wider recurring adjusted-profit gap; a material undisclosed case; Material/Disqualifying controller/KMP fact; audit or ICFR weakness | Verified registry/beneficial-owner and legal-screen package for Rua California and the named Junqueira/Pereira holder vehicles; itemised 2026 AGM votes; FY26 executive scorecards; FY26 share-count and adjusted-profit bridges |

## 7. Note To The Final Synthesizer

- Stewardship is **Standard / mixed**: operating delivery and clean issuer audits are offset by weak minority rights and incomplete external coverage; the strongest evidence is Vélez’s 18.6% economic stake carrying 74.4% of votes while he is CEO and chair.
- **Non-Negotiable Gate: PASS with 114/179 checklist items answered.** No hard disqualifier, Critical flag, Disqualifying gate person, or material undisclosed legal matter is proven.
- Capital allocation has produced higher diluted EPS and an early end-period share reduction, but returns on Hyperplane, associates, Banco Porto Real and software/market expansion are not disclosed.
- Employee equity is broad and a clawback exists, but executive metrics, weights and individual payouts are opaque; economic ownership does not overcome the super-vote wedge.
- People read: 24 Clean on own record, 0 Material, 0 Disqualifying; every grade is coverage-limited. The highest-risk item is unswept holder-vehicle/entity coverage, not an open §24 Filter 1 signal.
- Checklist read: 63.7% answered; Reds are A1-02, A1-03, A3-03, A3-04, A4-02 and A4-13. Legal/regulatory risk at 40 is the binding Checklist Risk.
- Biggest formal flags: RF-AUD-007 High, non-gate; RF-DISC-002 Medium, non-gate. Structural founder control is the biggest governance risk but is not itself a misconduct finding.
- Business-model/01 hard disqualifier: **none; “Any disqualifier triggered: N.”**
- §24 rejector filters: turnaround N; serial acquirer N; structurally unaligned owner RF-OWN-004 N on available evidence; unresolved integrity signal N. No rejector-filter cap applies.
- Partial-data caps: legal/person/database coverage limits confidence to 70 and floors Legal risk at 40; query-budget exhaustion cuts People & network integrity from 58 to 55. Actual confidence is 68.
- Highest-value next data request: a verified corporate-registry and beneficial-owner extract, followed by identifier-based court/regulator/sanctions checks, for Rua California Ltd. and the disclosed Junqueira/Pereira holder vehicles.
- **This module supersedes business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance. Treat this module’s verdict and scores as the primary governance read.**

## 8. Simple Summary

- Management met the small number of mature promises, but there is not enough formal guidance to call the forecasting record proven.
- Diluted EPS rose 175.6% from FY23 to FY25; the new buyback lowered end-period shares, but full-year diluted shares have not yet fallen.
- Pay uses broad equity and a clawback, but investors cannot see individual executive measures, weights or payouts.
- Insiders own 22.80% on the vendor measure; Vélez has 18.6% of the economics and 74.4% of votes. Open-market activity was four sales and no buys.
- Minority protection is weak because the founder is CEO/chair and controls votes, nominations and major-action consents.
- Management was candid about one favorable timing distortion, but adjusted profit still excludes recurring stock compensation.
- No hard disqualifier or Critical red flag was found.
- The module is useful, but its Watchlist rating carries a coverage cap: person, entity and legal sweeps are incomplete.
